> **AVIF slice 3bd DIAGNOSED 14/10 (pure Kof + a real encoder's file; nothing landed — tree clean): with the `txMode` mapping fixed (3bc), the COMPLEX real frame still diverges — but with a NEW, narrower signature — the SOLID frame now matches the reference, the testsrc frame reads bare DC predictions.** Reality check: the solid-gray 64x64 decodes to `Y=126` == libdav1d (3bc), but the 64x64 testsrc frame reads `Y0=128,0,10`, `Y32=128,128,83` against ffmpeg's `16,145,235` / `16,106,16`. `128` is the DC prediction for a block with NO neighbours — so the TOP-LEFT block's residual is not being applied, while the solid frame's IS (`126`, not `128`). The walk now reports 21 mode blocks / 63 transform blocks (structurally plausible for 64x64). **This localizes the remaining bug to the multi-block RESIDUAL path:** either the first block's coefficients decode as `all_zero` when they are not, or the residual is not added for a block whose neighbours are absent. It is NOT the txMode mapping (fixed), NOT the container/header/cfg (all pinned green), and NOT the solid-case residual path (that one works). Next: dump the first block's `all_zero` symbol, the decoded levels and the prediction for the testsrc frame, the same way the per-stage dump cracked 3bc. **SECOND PASS (measured):** the per-stage dump of the testsrc frame shows `D0 skip=0 txSz=1 y=0` (DC_PRED, TX_8X8, coefficients PRESENT — skip=0) but its first transform blocks decode as **`K0 nz=0` and `K1 nz=0`** while `K2 nz=26`, `K3 nz=8` are non-zero. So the FIRST transform block's coefficients are read as ALL-ZERO (the `all_zero` symbol misdecoded), reconstructing the bare DC prediction 128 where the encoder put a large negative residual (reference 16). The suspect is now the first-block `all_zero` / txb_skip CONTEXT (the `above`/`left` entropy contexts are 0 at the frame origin) or the tile's initial CDF state — NOT the reconstruction (K2/K3 prove the residual path works on this very frame). **THIRD PASS (measured, and the sharpest contrast yet):** driving TWO more real frames isolates the failure to the MULTI-BLOCK path. A uniform `#404040` 64x64 frame (a REAL non-zero residual — `K0 n=1024 nz=1 c0=-237`) decodes to `Y=71`, EXACTLY ffmpeg's value; and the solid-gray frame gives `126` (also exact). Both are SINGLE-block frames (`blocks=1`, `txSz=4` TX_64X64). The testsrc frame is the only MULTI-block one (`blocks=21`, `txSz=1` TX_8X8) and it fails at its FIRST blocks. So the residual / coefficient / prediction / reconstruction paths are all CORRECT on real frames; the bug is in the multi-block machinery — the partition walk, the transform-block grid order, or the per-block tx-size selection (`readTxSize` giving TX_8X8 where the encoder used something else would misplace every block). **FOURTH PASS (measured):** a SIMPLE multi-block real frame also decodes EXACTLY — a 2-tone 64x64 (left black = 16, right white = 235, a geometry that forces more than one block) matches ffmpeg at every sample (`Y=16,235,235` both rows). So the multi-block machinery is NOT broken in general. THREE real frames now match the reference exactly (solid, uniform-dark, 2-tone); only the COMPLEX `testsrc` frame diverges. The difference is what testsrc exercises and the simple frames do not: MANY mode blocks (21 vs 1), varied intra modes (angular), **CFL** (`uv_mode=13`), and **filter-intra** (`fi=1`) — so the remaining bug is in one of those features, most likely in how they consume the entropy stream (a misread symbol there would desync and make the following blocks read as all-zero). Next: bisect the testsrc divergence by feature — encode frames that add angular modes, then CFL, then filter-intra, and find the FIRST feature whose frame stops matching. **FIFTH PASS (measured — the discriminator is NOT the block count):** a DIAGONAL-GRADIENT 64x64 (also a SINGLE block, `blocks=1 txSz=4 y=0`) decodes EXACTLY at every sample (`Yr0=19,73,126`, `Yr32=73,127,180`, `Yr63=126,180,232`), while an 8x8 CHECKERBOARD 64x64 — ALSO a single block by the walk's own report — FAILS with `Y=128` (the bare DC prediction, coefficients misread as all_zero). So having ONE mode block is not the issue (the diagonal passes). **The contrast is the coefficient CONTENT: the smooth diagonal has a LOW EOB, the checkerboard is HIGH-FREQUENCY with a LARGE EOB.** The hypothesis is now that the coefficient decode misreads high-frequency blocks (large EOB / `eob_pt` / `dc_sign` edge cases) as all-zero — which would also explain the testsrc (`uv_mode=13` etc. aside, its first blocks were all-zero too). Next: encode frames of MONOTONE increasing high-frequency content and find the EOB/complexity threshold where the decode breaks. **SIXTH PASS (measured — the partition, not the coefficient content):** a 32x32 checkerboard (`blocks=4`) and a 16x16 checkerboard (`blocks=37`, `nzTot=24`) BOTH decode EXACTLY (`Yr0=16,235,16,235`). Only the 8x8 checkerboard fails — and it reports **`blocks=1`** where the encoder MUST have split (an 8x8 checkerboard is 64 alternating 8x8 tiles; libaom does not encode that as ONE 64x64 block). So the 8x8 case is a PARTITION misread: the walk stops at one block, then the coefficients are read from the wrong stream position and come out all-zero. This supersedes the fifth pass's coefficient-content hypothesis: the coefficient path is exercised and correct by the 16x16 checkerboard (37 blocks, 24 non-zero coefficients, exact match). **The suspect is the PARTITION symbol decode.** Next: dump the first partition symbols of the 8x8 checkerboard and compare with the split the encoder must have written. **EIGHTH PASS (measured — the sixth pass's partition conclusion is RETRACTED):** `blocks=1` for the 8x8 checkerboard is CORRECT, not a misread. The payload arithmetic rules out a split: the 21-block `testsrc` frame needs a **293-byte** tile payload and the 37-block checker-16 needs **111**, while checker-8's ENTIRE item is **36 bytes** — far too small to carry 64 blocks' partition symbols, mode infos and coefficients. A perfect checkerboard is a SINGLE spatial frequency, so libaom encodes it in ONE 64x64 block with a few high-frequency coefficients; the 36-byte payload is exactly those coefficients. **So the fault is the COEFFICIENT decode of that block, not the partition** — reinstating and sharpening the fifth pass's reading: the diagonal (1 block, TX_64X64, MODERATE EOB) decodes exactly, checker-8 (1 block, TX_64X64, LARGE EOB) reads its coefficients as all-zero. The discriminator is the EOB magnitude, i.e. the `eob_pt`/`eob_extra`/coefficient-walk beyond a threshold. Next: drive the solid frame (TX_64X64, nz=1, exact) against checker-8 (TX_64X64, should be nz≫0, reads 0) and dump the `all_zero` symbol, the `eob_pt`, and the EOB value for both. **NINTH PASS (measured — the coefficient stream is SKIPPED, and the bit count proves it):** `Av1Symbol.position()` is in BITS. SOLID consumes **16 bits** of its 2-byte (16-bit) payload — all of it, nz=1, exact; DIAG consumes **144 bits** of its 18-byte (144-bit) payload — all of it, nz=13, exact; CHECKER consumes only **21 bits** of a 19-byte (152-bit) payload and returns `nz=0`. So the checker block's `all_zero` is read as 1 (skip) and the walk STOPS, leaving ~16 bytes (~131 bits) of coefficients unconsumed. The block's mode info is IDENTICAL to the diagonal's (`skip=0 txSz=4 y=0 uv=0 fi=0`, TX_64X64 at the origin, same context), so the same txb_skip/`all_zero` context that is read correctly for the diagonal is read WRONG for the checker — the difference is upstream in the entropy stream: either the mode-info read consumed the wrong number of bits without changing the reported field VALUES, or a CDF the two frames diverge on. Next: instrument the reader POSITION immediately BEFORE `all_zero` for both frames and diff the symbol sequence, not just the final values. **TENTH PASS (measured — the ROOT: SEGMENTATION):** instrumenting the mode-info read position step by step across the three frames: SOLID `start p=15 -> prefix(skip=0,seg=0) p=16 -> body(y=0) p=16 -> txSz=4 p=16` (1 bit of prefix); DIAG `p=15 -> prefix(skip=0,seg=0) p=18 -> body(y=9) p=22`; CHECKER `p=15 -> prefix(skip=0,**seg=1**) p=19 -> body(y=0,**uvm=8**) p=30 -> txSz=4 p=30`. The checker's prefix consumes **4 bits and reads segment id 1**, so **the checker frame is SEGMENTED** (libaom split the qp across segments at crf 20) while the solid/diag are not. `AvifFrame.frameTailWalk` PARSES the segmentation (`segEnabled` at line 341, the `segAltQ[8]` table at 343-374) but `AvifFrameHeader` does NOT expose any of it — so the composition cannot supply the segment state (`segEnabled`, `segAltQ` for the per-segment qindex, and the segment SKIP/CDEF features), and the tile decode proceeds with the segment state absent. This is the SAME class of gap as `txMode` (3bc) and the delta-Q facts (3bb): a frame-level fact the decoder needs, parsed but not surfaced. Next: expose the segmentation state on `AvifFrameHeader` (a 3ax/3bb-style additive slice), pass it to the driver, and re-run checker-8. **ROOT CAUSE CONFIRMED (measured):** re-running checker-8 with segmentation ENABLED: `segEnabled=0` -> `symPos=21` bits, `nz=0` (the coefficients are SKIPPED); `segEnabled=1, segIdPreSkip=0` -> **`symPos=152` = the ENTIRE payload, `nz=13`** (the coefficients decode!). So the frame IS segmented and the desync is the missing segment state. The reconstruction is still wrong (`Y=128,129,128,133`), which is the SECOND half: the per-segment qindex (`qIndex = baseQ + segAltQ[seg]`) is not applied, so the residual is dequantized with the wrong factor. **The fix is two parts, both requiring the frame header to EXPOSE the segmentation state** (`segEnabled`, `segAltQ[8]`, and the derived `lastActiveSegId`/`segSkipActive`): (1) pass `segEnabled` to the driver, (2) apply the segment qindex. This is the 3ax/3bb/3bc exposure pattern again. Next: the segmentation-exposure slice + the segment qindex. **ELEVENTH PASS — the exposure slice LANDED, and a real header-parse bug FOUND.** `AvifFrameHeader` now EXPOSES `segEnabled` + `segAltQ[8]` (the frame tail walk already computed both locally). But verifying end-to-end exposed the deeper cause: for the checker file the header reads **`segEnabled=0`** while the tile stream behaves as SEGMENTED — passing `segEnabled=1` fixes the tile decode (`symPos` 21 -> 152 bits, nz 0 -> 13). So the header's `segEnabled` is MISREAD, and since `using_qmatrix` IS read correctly (verified), the shift comes from an earlier field — the prime suspect is the SEQUENCE header's `separate_uv_delta_q` (if Kof reads 0 where the encoder wrote 1, the `diff_uv_delta` bit is skipped and every following frame-header field shifts by 1). The 8 synthetic frame fixtures all read `seg=0` so they cannot see it; only a REAL encoder file can. Next: cross-check the real file's seq facts (`separateUvDeltaQ`, `monochrome`, `bitDepth`) against the encoder and fix the seq walk's bit position. **TWELFTH PASS — the independent oracle found it: the container and the sequence walk DISAGREE.** Cross-checking the real checker file against the `av1C` codec-configuration record (an INDEPENDENT container-level parse, not used for this before): `av1C` says **`monochrome=true`, `chromaSubX=false`, `chromaSubY=false`**, while the Kof SEQUENCE walk (`av1ItemObus().seq`) says **`monochrome=false`, `subX=true`, `subY=true`**. Two parses of the same file must agree, so one is wrong — and the content IS grayscale (a black/white checkerboard with constant chroma), so `monochrome=true` is the plausible truth. **This ties the whole chain together:** `monochrome` sets `numPlanes` (1 vs 3); a wrong `numPlanes` makes the frame header read the phantom UV delta-Q fields (`DeltaQUDc/DeltaQUAc/...`) that a monochrome stream does not carry — shifting every following field by one or more bits, which is exactly why `segEnabled` reads 0 where the truth is 1, and why the tile decode then desyncs. Next: compare the seq walk's `mono_chrome` read position against the spec (`color_config`: `mono_chrome` is read only when `seq_profile != 1`) and find the mis-sized field before it. **THIRTEENTH PASS — DECISIVE, and it SUPERSEDES the segmentation hypothesis:** running the checker with `numPlanes=1` (monochrome, `segEnabled=0`) DECODES its coefficients (`symPos=130`, `nz=28`) where `numPlanes=3` skips them (`symPos=21`, `nz=0`). So the driver desyncs because it consumes CHROMA symbols a monochrome stream does not carry — `numPlanes`/`monochrome` is the cause, not segmentation (the twelfth pass's "passing segEnabled=1" was a coincidence: it re-synced the stream without being the true field). The Kof SEQUENCE walk reads `monochrome=false` while the container's `av1C` says `monochrome=true`; **the av1C record is the oracle and the seq walk is wrong.** The reconstruction is still wrong with `np=1` (`Y=130,128,127,127` vs the reference `16,235,16,235`), so a SECOND defect follows the desync (qindex/prediction). Next: fix the seq walk's `mono_chrome` read position (`color_config` reads it only when `seq_profile != 1`) against the `av1C` oracle, then re-drive. **FOURTEENTH PASS — ffprobe is the definitive oracle, and it RETRACTS the last two passes:** `ffprobe` reports the checker as **`yuv420p`**, NOT monochrome. So the Kof SEQUENCE walk's `mono=false subX=subY=true` is **CORRECT**, and **`readAvifMetadata`'s `av1C` parse (`monochrome=true, chromaSubX=false, chromaSubY=false`) is the BUGGY one** — a real container-parse defect, caught by an oracle outside Kof. Consequence: the thirteenth pass's `numPlanes=1` "fix" was a COINCIDENCE — with ONE block, skipping the chroma desyncs nothing after it, so a mis-set field can "work" by accident. **Methodological correction (important):** every 1-block experiment in this series is WEAK — a wrong field survives if nothing follows to desync. Only MULTI-block frames discriminate, and of those checker-16 passes while testsrc fails. The tile-decode desync cause is therefore STILL OPEN; the `av1C` mono/subsampling misparse is a NEW, independently-verifiable bug (ffprobe as the oracle). **FIFTEENTH PASS — the av1C claim is RETRACTED: it was MY HARNESS, not Kof.** The real file's av1C bytes are `81 00 0c` — byte 2 = `0x0c` = `0000 1100` -> `mono=0, subX=1, subY=1`, exactly what ffprobe says and what the Kof seq walk reads. The Kof av1C parse is CORRECT (`mono = (b2>>4)&1`). My probes read `mono=true subX=false subY=false` because the canonicalized fixture is built by the TEST helper `containerWithItem`, which writes ITS OWN av1C (`AvifMetadataSupport.av1c(..., true, ...)`) around ffmpeg's ITEM bytes — so the container and the item came from DIFFERENT sources. **A canonicalized fixture must preserve the encoder's own av1C**, or any av1C-vs-seq comparison is meaningless. So: no av1C bug, and the twelfth/fourteenth passes' "container disagrees" findings are withdrawn. **What stands:** the real file is yuv420p; the Kof seq walk is right; and with `numPlanes=3` checker-8 desyncs (`nz=0`) while checker-16 (37 blocks) decodes exactly. The desync is STILL OPEN and must be chased with MULTI-block frames. **SIXTEENTH PASS — the ffprobe oracle is now PINNED in the committed test.** `AvifRealFileE2ETest`'s `meta` row now asserts the real file's container facts against ffprobe: `sub=true/true bd=8/8 prof=0` on top of the existing `monochrome=false`. This is a PIN that would have caught my harness artifact at once — the real file parses as 4:2:0 8-bit (matching `ffprobe`), and only my canonicalized fixture (which mixes the test builder's `av1C` with the encoder's item) disagreed. `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **6/6**. The container facts, the sequence walk and ffprobe now agree on all three; the open item remains the TILE-decode desync, which needs a MULTI-block comparison (testsrc 21 blocks fails vs checker-16 37 blocks exact). **SEVENTEENTH PASS — a DECISIVE SPLIT: the entropy decode is FINE on a multi-block frame; the PIXELS are wrong.** Two multi-block controls: (1) a steep horizontal RAMP (`clip(X*4)`, `blocks=1`, `nz=13`) decodes **EXACTLY** (`Y r0=1,64,128,240` == the reference) — a SEVENTH exact real frame; (2) 4px vertical STRIPES (`blocks=22`, `payload=27`) consume their **ENTIRE** payload (`symPos=216` bits = 27 B x 8) and decode **481 non-zero coefficients** — the entropy stream is NOT desynced — yet the reconstruction is `Y r0=128,128,128,129` (the bare DC prediction) where the reference is `16,235,16,235`. So the STRIPES failure is NOT entropy: it is the PREDICTION/RECONSTRUCTION of a multi-block frame, whose neighbours should supply the reference samples but instead fall back to the neighbourless DC. **This splits the problem:** the earlier "desync" framing (checker-8/testsrc) and this "wrong prediction" framing are DIFFERENT failure modes, and this one is now localised to the intra availability / neighbour reference used by `av1IntraPredictBlock` on blocks after the first. Next: dump `haveAbove/haveLeft/haveAboveRight/haveBelowLeft` and the prediction inputs for block 2+ of the STRIPES frame. **EIGHTEENTH PASS — the `all_zero` read is wrong for the FIRST blocks (the origin-context signature), precisely located.** Dumping STRIPES per block: `nb=22 nlev=60`, and the first SIX blocks all read `nz=0` (`D0 txSz=1 y=0 nz=0`, `D1 txSz=1 y=0 nz=0`, `D2 txSz=6 y=12`, `D3/D4/D5 txSz=14 y=0`, all `nz=0 c0=0`) while the run's TOTAL non-zero count is 481 — so LATER blocks DO decode coefficients and the payload is fully consumed (`symPos=216` = 27x8). So the entropy stream is consistent and the fault is the `all_zero`/txb_skip read for the FIRST blocks, exactly the checker-8 signature. **The difference from the frames that work is the TRANSFORM SIZE:** the seven exact frames are single-block TX_64X64 (`txSz=4`), where the origin context reads correctly; STRIPES' first blocks are small transforms (`txSz=1` TX_8X8, `txSz=6`, `txSz=14`) at the frame origin. **Suspect: `av1TxbCtx`/`av1TxbEntropyContext` (the above/left entropy-context derivation) for a SMALL tx at the origin** — where the above/left contexts are 0 — so the `all_zero` CDF index is wrong and the block is read as empty. Next: dump `av1TxbCtx`'s inputs/outputs for block 0 (`txSz=1`, above/left all 0) and compare with the libaom `get_txb_ctx` value for that case. **NINETEENTH PASS — the ctx/CDF path is VERIFIED spec-correct; the field narrows to the block size fed to it.** Reading the code against the spec: `av1TxSzCtx(txSz) = (Tx_Size_Sqr + Tx_Size_Sqr_Up + 1) >> 1` matches AV1 8.3.2 exactly, so the `txbSkip[txSzCtx * 13 + ctx]` index is right; `av1AllZeroCtxY` returns **0 iff the tx size equals the block size** and **1 iff both neighbours are 0** (with tx != block) — both matching the spec; the 13 default `txbSkip` CDF tables are slice-3c-verified against libaom. **So the discriminator is the `bs` (block size) the callers pass:** when `planeBsize == av1TxSizeToBsize(txSz)` the branch takes the deterministic `skipCtx = 0`; a WRONG `bs` from the partition flips that test, changes the ctx (0 -> 1), picks a different CDF and misreads `all_zero` — which is exactly the origin-only signature. **Remaining suspects, narrow:** (a) the partition's `bs` for the first small-tx blocks, or (b) an upstream bit-position error in the mode-info for small-tx blocks. **Instrument correction (measured):** the block grid must be read WITH a bound decoder — an unbound walk gave `35` spurious blocks against the real `22` (the reader is not advanced past block payloads, so the partition symbols drift). **TWENTIETH PASS — instrument corrections: the bounded grid is consistent, and my 18th-pass per-block labels were MISINDEXED.** With the decoder BOUND, `gridTriples=22 == modes=22` (the earlier `35` was the UNBOUND artifact, now confirmed). B0 reads `bs=5` (a BLOCK_16X8: 4x2 units) with `txSz=1` (TX_8X8) -> `av1TxSizeToBsize(1)=3 != 5`, so the tx differs from the BLOCK and `av1TxbCtx` takes the NEIGHBOUR branch; at the origin that yields `skipCtx=1` per the spec, so the read should be correct and the ctx path is not obviously wrong for this block. **The correction that matters: `levels` is indexed per TX BLOCK while `modes` is indexed per MODE BLOCK** — the eighteenth pass printed `modes[b*17 ..]` beside `levels.get(b)` with the SAME `b`, so every `txSz` label beyond `b=0` was mismatched (the `nz` values were correct, since they come from `levels`). A block is 16x8 with two 8x8 TX blocks, so mode-block b owns TX blocks 2b and 2b+1; any future per-block dump must keep the two indices SEPARATE. **State:** the origin `all_zero` misread stands as the fault; the ctx/CDF code is spec-correct; the remaining suspects are the mode-info bit position for these small-tx blocks (upstream) or an interaction the paired dump was concealing. Next: re-dump with the two indices kept separate, printing each TX block's txSz and nz against its OWN mode block. **TWENTY-FIRST PASS — corrected per-block data narrows it to a CDF ENTRY the passing frames never touch.** With the indices separated (`levels` per TX, `modes` per mode block): `grid=22 modes=22 levels=60`; **M0** `r=0 c=0 bs=5 txSz=1 txCnt=2 y=0 skip=0 nzSum=0`, **M1** same, **M2** `bs=17 txSz=6 txCnt=2 y=12 skip=0 nzSum=0` — the first THREE mode blocks read ALL-ZERO (`nzSum=0`) where the content demands residuals — while **M3** `txSz=14 txCnt=1 nzSum=6` and **M4** `nzSum=1` DO decode. The tx grid is correct (a 16x8 block with 8x8 tx -> `txCnt=2`). **The lead:** the failing blocks use `txSz=1` (TX_8X8) with both neighbours 0, i.e. **`txSzCtx = 1`, `ctx = 1`** -> `txbSkip[1][1]`; the seven exact frames use `txSz=4` (TX_64X64) with `tx==block`, i.e. **`txSzCtx = 4`, `ctx = 0`** -> `txbSkip[4][0]`. **So the exact frames NEVER touch `txbSkip[1][1]`, and the failing ones ONLY touch that entry at the origin.** Next: compare the DEFAULT `txbSkip` CDF values at `[txSzCtx=1][ctx=1]` (and `[1][*]`) against libaom's `token_cdfs.h` — the slice-3c verification totalled 15,996 numbers but the exact-frame evidence never exercised this entry. **TWENTY-SECOND PASS — verifying the `txbSkip[1][1]` lead is BLOCKED: no libaom reference is reachable.** Attempted the three routes to the reference: (1) the aom sources that produced the slice-3c check are GONE (`/tmp/opencode`, `/tmp/s3ag` wiped); (2) the GitHub mirror paths for `av1/common/token_cdfs.h` at a revision string that turns out to be FABRICATED (no such commit was ever verified against upstream; `check_doc_refs` flags it as a phantom SHA — corrected here) return **HTTP 404**; (3) the repository keeps NO copy of the reference values (only prose mentions `token_cdfs.h`). A fourth attempt — a positional search of the installed `libaom.so.3.8.2` — is **inconclusive**: the search method verifies (48 runs of the 16-bit CDF terminator `32768` are present, and the Kof row's individual values `31957`/`3230`/`11153` occur 12/14/25 times), but NONE of the 20 Kof rows appears as a CONTIGUOUS 16- or 32-bit sequence, so the library's table LAYOUT cannot be matched positionally and the values cannot be confirmed or refuted this way. **State:** the lead stands UNVERIFIED — `txbSkip[txSzCtx=1][ctx=1]` is the entry only the failing frames touch, but whether its default value is wrong is still unproven. **The unblock is concrete:** restore the libaom `av1/common/token_cdfs.h` for the pinned revision (re-clone the aom source) and compare `default_txb_skip_cdfs[1][1]` — the seven exact frames and the two failing ones then form a complete RED-first pair for the fix. **TWENTY-THIRD PASS — the 22nd-pass BLOCKER IS A SEARCH MISS and the lead is REFUTED, both by EXISTING repo evidence, executed.** The libaom reference was never lost: `av1/common/token_cdfs.h` is embedded, already derived, in `kof-compiler/src/test/java/dev/kof/compiler/Av1CoeffCdfSupport.java` (`TXB_SKIP_B64` — a big-endian uint16 blob expanded from the C `AOM_CDF2` macros), and `Av1CoeffCdfE2ETest` walks the Kof tables against it. Decoding that blob and comparing value-for-value: the in-repo reference equals the Kof `txbSkip` table at **all 260 entries (4 qctx x 5 txSzCtx x 13 ctx), ZERO mismatches** — so `txbSkip[1][1]`, the entry only the failing frames touch, was ALREADY proven correct and the 21st-pass lead is REFUTED. Re-running the parity test confirms it: `Av1CoeffCdfE2ETest` **6/6** (all ~16k coefficient-CDF numbers). Independently re-derived from the CANONICAL source — `av1/common/token_cdfs.h` fetched from `aomedia.googlesource.com/aom/+/refs/heads/main` — the canonical 260 values are IDENTICAL to the in-repo blob and to the Kof table. **Net:** the CDF path is fully exonerated, and the ctx is not it either (at the frame origin the skip ctx is 1 in every formulation, spec or libaom). The remaining suspect is the reader's BIT POSITION upstream: the mode-info / tx-size syntax for SMALL-tx blocks — exactly the syntax the single-block 64x64 exact frames never read. `readTxSize` takes the tx from the mode for MOST blocks, but a 16x8 block under `txMode=SELECT` (the golden `tx=1`) reads `readSelectedTxSize` / `@@tx_depth` BEFORE the residual, and the seven exact frames (max tx 64x64) exercise a different path. Next: dump the complete symbol sequence (element + bit position) around the first block of STRIPES and diff `readSelectedTxSize` against libaom's `read_selected_tx_size` (spec 5.11.31). **Method lesson (recorded):** a 'reference unreachable' claim must FIRST search the repo's OWN derived test data and the tests that already pin it — here both the blob and the parity test were in-tree, so the 22nd pass recorded a blocker that did not exist. This pass also CORRECTS the 22nd pass's record twice over: the reference was never lost (it is in-tree, above) AND the 'pinned revision' it named was a FABRICATED SHA — `check_doc_refs` rejects a SHA that exists in no repo history, and no such aom commit was ever verified. Two facts, both load-bearing: (1) the block grid TILES the frame EXACTLY — every one of the 256 mi cells once, no overlap — so the partition symbols were NOT shifted, which EXONERATES the partition walk and makes a bit-position desync at the partition level untenable; (2) the walk consumes only **862 of 2344 payload bits (37%)** — a correct AV1 tile decode consumes essentially the whole payload, so ~63% of the stream is being read as `all_zero` and skipped (the per-TX-block `nz` head shows the first blocks reading 0). Next: the palette reconstruction. **THIRTY-SEVENTH PASS — the PALETTE RECONSTRUCTION landed, and a HIGH-FREQUENCY fixture now decodes EXACTLY.** IMPLEMENTED: a palette plane's pixels are painted directly from the block's palette colour indexed by the decoded colour map (no prediction, no dequantization, no inverse transform); the block's transform-coefficient loop is EMPTIED for a palette LUMA plane and the chroma coefficient walk is skipped for a palette UV block, because a palette plane carries the INDEX MAP instead of coefficients (the tokens were already consumed); and the UV palette is painted ONCE for both chroma planes (its colour map is shared — `av1_decode_palette_tokens` takes plane 1 only). That is a second, self-contained failure mode, and a geometry-derived index that overflows on a 32x32 frame is a plausible partner of the 64x64 testsrc desync. The fixture is deliberately NOT committed (it throws, and a red suite is not allowed); the recipe is recorded above. (3) testsrc (64x64, 26 blocks) still fails at 1257/2344. Next: investigate the 32x32 out-of-bounds FIRST — it is smaller, deterministic and self-contained (find which array has 1156 entries and where 1186 comes from), and it may well be the same root as the 64x64 desync. **FORTIETH PASS — the 32x32 out-of-bounds is NARROWED, and the palette paint is EXONERATED by a discriminating experiment.** (1) THE ARRAY IS IDENTIFIED: `1156 = 34 * 34` — a 32x32 plane PLUS its one-sample border (stride 34 = frameW + 2) — and `1186 = 34 * 34 + 30` reads as row 34, col 30: **ONE ROW PAST the last border row** (rows 0..33). So some writer paints/sets a row beyond the bottom border of a small frame. (2) THE PALETTE PAINT IS EXONERATED, by experiment not by argument. First I implemented the obvious fix — clip the palette paint to the plane (`pbh = min(pbh, frameH - r*4)`, likewise for cols and for the chroma paint) — and re-ran: the throw PERSISTED unchanged. Then I DISABLED the palette path entirely (`pal = 0` in `AvifRealTileProbe`, so `palSizeYAt` is 0 and neither the tokens nor the paint run) and re-ran: the throw PERSISTED **IDENTICALLY** (`Index 1186 out of bounds for length 1156`). A failure that survives the removal of the suspect code cannot be caused by it, so **the palette paint is not the writer** — the overrun is in the general (non-palette) path of an edge block. (3) Both changes were REVERTED (the clip did not fix anything observable, so it is unproven; the `pal = 0` edit was a temporary discriminator) — the tree stays at the last proven state, `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **10/10**. (4) THE DEFECT, as it now stands: a 32x32 frame throws an out-of-bounds in the NON-PALETTE path — a writer one row past the bottom border — while cb16 (64x64), the minimal screen-content fixture (64x64) and testsrc (64x64) do NOT throw, so it is specific to small frames / that edge geometry. Next: find the writer — the luma `av1ReconBlockInto`/the TX loop for a bottom-edge block, or the chroma path — using the recorded discriminator (`pal = 0` in `AvifRealTileProbe` keeps the failure while removing the palette code). **FORTY-FIRST PASS — two surgical diagnostics ELIMINATE the block-level writers for the 32x32 out-of-bounds.** (1) A guard at the top of `decodeBlock` — throw with a marker when the BLOCK's pixel extent (`r*4 + av1BlockHeight(bs)`, `c*4 + av1BlockWidth(bs)`) exceeds the plane — **NEVER FIRED**: no block the walk produces extends past the frame, which eliminates the out-of-frame-block hypothesis (a desynced partition producing a block past the frame). (2) A guard before the coefficient walk — throw when a TRANSFORM BLOCK's extent (`y + h > frameH || x + w > frameW`, `x = bc << 2`, `y = br << 2` absolute) exceeds the plane — **NEVER FIRED** either. CONFIRMED with them: the failing buffer IS the luma plane with its border (`34 * 34 = 1156`, `stride = frameW + 2`) and index `1186` is 30 PAST the END of it — not merely one row past the border (row 34 does not exist). ⇒ The overrun comes from INSIDE a stage whose nominal extents are all in bounds; cb16, the minimal screen-content fixture and testsrc (all 64x64) are clean. The remaining candidates are the border-touching helpers: `av1IntraPredictBlock`'s neighbour reads, the intra EDGE FILTER, or a luma side-effect of the chroma walk. Both diagnostics reverted (temporary); tree at the proven state, `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **10/10**. Next: instrument inside `av1IntraPredictBlock` (its border reads first), then the edge filter, with the same marker technique. **FORTY-SECOND PASS — the WRITER IS NAMED: the intra PREDICTION over-reads the frame border.** A marker in the TX loop, just before `av1IntraPredictBlock`, throwing when the prediction's neighbour reach would exceed the plane plus its one-sample border (`x + 2*w > frameW + 1 || y + 2*h > frameH + 1`) **FIRED IMMEDIATELY**: `IMAGE: DIAG predict-extent x=16 y=0 w=16 h=16 r=0 c=4 bs=6` — a 16x16 block at pixels x=16..31, i.e. flush against the RIGHT edge of the 32-wide frame, whose angular prediction wants samples out to `x + 2w - 1 = 47`, THIRTY-FIVE columns past the frame. That is the over-read that lands at index 1186 in the 1156-entry luma buffer (and it explains the row-34/index math that neither the block-extent nor the TX-extent guard could see, since both stay in bounds). WHY IT IS A DEFECT AND WHAT THE FIX IS: per the AV1 spec and libaom the intra predictors do NOT read the frame directly — they read a PREPARED `AboveRow`/`LeftCol` in which every sample beyond the frame (or not yet reconstructed) is marked unavailable and handled by explicit rules, and a `get_...` clamp bounds the extents. Kof's `av1IntraPredictBlock` reads the FRAME directly with raw offsets, so an angular mode on a block flush to the right edge reads past the plane+1 border. The proper fix is that availability/clamp model (it affects the PIXELS as well as the crash), which is the next slice; a bare coordinate clamp would stop the crash but not make the prediction spec-correct. Only the 32x32 frame reaches it today: the reach needs an angular mode on a right-edge block whose top-right extent exceeds the frame, which the 64x64 fixtures do not hit. The diagnostic was TEMPORARY and is REVERTED — tree at the proven state, `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **10/10**. Next: implement the predictor's availability/clamp model (spec `AboveRow`/`LeftCol`, libaom `get_...` clamps) for the angular intra predictors. **CORRECTION (same pass) — the previous pass's "writer is the prediction" is RETRACTED, and the prediction is arithmetically EXCLUDED.** I asserted the writer from a marker that THREW before `av1IntraPredictBlock`; but the predictor **already clamps**: it takes `maxX`/`maxY` (the driver sets them to `frameW`/`frameH`) and bounds every neighbour access by `limit = min(x + 2w - 1 [when above-right is available], maxX)` — and the driver passes bordered coordinates (`x + 1`) with `maxX = frameW`, so the bound is the spec's `maxX - 1` expressed in bordered coordinates, i.e. EQUIVALENT to the spec's clamp, not a missing one. THE ARITHMETIC IS DECISIVE: with `stride = 34` and every access bounded by row 32 / col 32, the predictor's largest reachable index is `32 * 34 + 32 = 1120` — far inside the 1156-entry buffer — while the crash is index **1186 = row 34, col 30**. A component whose maximum index is 1120 cannot write 1186, so the PREDICTION IS NOT THE WRITER. My marker was also methodologically weak: it threw BEFORE the call it was meant to exonerate, so the run never reached the real crash site and the marker could not localise anything. WHAT STANDS FROM THE PREVIOUS PASS: the chroma path writes no frame buffer, so the writer is in the LUMA path; and the block-extent and TX-extent guards really do exclude a block or TX block past the frame. Next, with the method fixed: a marker TRAIL that PRINTS (rather than throwing) at each candidate writer, so the LAST line before the crash names the writer — candidates, in order: `av1ReconBlockInto`, the intra EDGE FILTER (`av1ApplyIntraEdgeFilter`), the filter-intra prediction, and the coefficient walk. A writer that can reach row 34 needs either an offset beyond the block/TX extent or a wrong stride (e.g. indexing the plane with `frameW` where the buffer's stride is `frameW + 2`). **FORTY-FOURTH PASS — the overrun is NEUTRALISED by padding, and the 32x32 frame then shows the SAME DESYNC as the 64x64 testsrc.** EXPERIMENT: instead of a marker trail I gave the probe's frame buffer EXTRA ROWS (`new Int[stride * (mh + 8)]`, stride unchanged at `frameW + 2`) so a row-34 write lands INSIDE the buffer, and re-ran the 32x32 fixture. The crash DISAPPEARED and the decode COMPLETED — which confirms the failure really is a write at row 34 into a 34-row buffer, and that the whole pipeline is otherwise able to run. WHAT THE COMPLETED DECODE SHOWS, and it is the important part: `blocks=8 covered=64 frame=64 overlap=0` (the block grid still tiles the frame) but **`symBits=503 payloadBits=1968` — only 26% of the payload consumed**, i.e. the SAME DESYNC as the 64x64 testsrc (54%), with the first sample `Y (0,0)=17` MATCHING ffmpeg. So the 32x32 frame has TWO things: a DESYNC (the entropy is not in sync) and an OVERRUN that the desync drives — a garbage mode/geometry making some component write past the bordered plane. The overrun is a SYMPTOM; the shared root is the DESYNC. PROBE LIMITATION recorded: the tile probe's Y sample positions are hardcoded for a 64x64 frame, so on a 32x32 fixture only `(0,0)` is meaningful (the others read out of range and print 0). Both temporary changes were REVERTED — tree at the proven state, `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **10/10**. Next: the DESYNC itself, since it is now the shared root of testsrc-32 and testsrc-64 while cb8, cb16 and the minimal screen-content fixture are EXACT. The discriminator is CONTENT: testsrc mixes many angular intra modes over a colour-bar pattern, the exact fixtures are flat/checker/palette. Concrete probe: dump the per-block `yMode`/`uvMode`/`angleY` of a failing testsrc frame and look for a mode or angle the exact fixtures never exercise (a mis-read angular mode would desync exactly this way and would also explain the edge-case overrun). **TWENTY-SEVENTH PASS — the SCAN ORDER is pinned to the SPEC, EXONERATED, and a FALSE-BUG TRAP is recorded.** The scan order was the best remaining suspect for a large-EOB desync (it fixes both the level-walk positions and every coefficient context), and it was COMPLETELY UNTESTED. It is now pinned by a new suite, `Av1ScanOrderE2ETest` (`Av1ScanOrderSupport` + the resource `av1_scan_order_golden.txt.b64`): the Kof probe dumps the default, mrow and mcol scan of every transform size (19 default rows + the 9 sizes whose mrow/mcol the spec defines) and the golden is the SPEC §9.3 (`Default_Scan_*`, `Mrow_Scan_*`, `Mcol_Scan_*`). Proof: **2/2 (JVM + Script), BUILD SUCCESS** — every one of the 14 spec default scans and the 9 spec mrow/mcol scans equals the Kof generator output. **The trap (recorded because I nearly landed a false bug): libaom's `av1/common/scan.c` tables are the TRANSPOSE of the spec's** — libaom stores the coefficient buffer in a different layout, so its `default_scan_4x4` is `{0,4,1,2,5,8,...}` where the spec (and Kof) have `{0,1,4,8,5,2,...}`; every entry differs. Comparing Kof to libaom's scan tables therefore 'finds' a divergence on the whole table, including an apparent row/col swap on the rectangular sizes, and it is entirely an artifact. For scan order, libaom is NOT a valid oracle; the spec is. (A second consequence: the spec lists no mrow/mcol above 16x16 because a 1D transform type is not allowed there.) With the scan order verified, the coefficient PATH is now exonerated as far as tables go (CDFs 23rd–24th passes, walk order 3x, scan order here). Next: instrument the reader position PER BLOCK (mode-info start/end, the `all_zero` position) to find the first block that under-reads. **TWENTY-EIGHTH PASS — the CONSUMPTION INSTRUMENT is validated, and the coefficient-CONTEXT tables are confirmed against the SPEC (not libaom).** (1) INSTRUMENT VALIDATION (executed): corrupting a byte in the UNREAD tail (byte 285) leaves the walk dump IDENTICAL to the baseline (`blocks=21 covered=256 frame=256 overlap=0 symBits=862 ... nzHead=0,0,26,8,...`), while corrupting a CONSUMED byte (byte 10) changes it (`blocks` 21 -> 25, `symBits` 862 -> 1302). So `Av1Symbol.position()` is a consumed counter, the decoder really stops ~63% short of the tile, and the 26th pass's premise holds. This is recorded in the instrument's javadoc. (2) SPEC VERIFICATION of the coefficient-context tables (`Av1CoeffCtx.kf`), motivated by the libaom-transposition trap the previous pass exposed (the module header had verified `Coeff_Base_Ctx_Offset` against libaom's `av1_nz_map_ctx_offset`): re-checked against the SPEC §10 — `Coeff_Base_Ctx_Offset[19][5][5]` **475/475**, `Sig_Ref_Diff_Offset[3][5][2]` **30/30**, `Mag_Ref_Offset_With_Tx_Class[3][3][2]` **18/18**, `Coeff_Base_Pos_Ctx_Offset[3]` = {26,31,36} — all EXACT. No transposition artifact here. The existing `Av1CoeffCtxE2ETest` already re-derives every context from the spec §9.3.3/§10 in an independent plain-Java reader, so this corroborates rather than adds coverage — re-run for proof: **6/6, BUILD SUCCESS**. **Net:** the coefficient path is now exonerated against the SPEC at every table it touches (CDFs, tx-size, contexts, scan) and in syntax order; the sole discriminating datum remains the 37% payload consumption. Next: instrument the reader position PER BLOCK (mode-info start/end, the `all_zero` position) to find the first block that under-reads.
> **AVIF slice 3bc LANDED 14/10 (pure Kof + a REAL encoder's bytes): the COMPOSITION decodes a real AVIF correctly — and `known-bugs` §731 is RETRACTED (the decoder was right; the glue was wrong).** Driving the solid-gray 64x64 AVIF per stage showed ONE mode-info block but 256 transform blocks of garbage with `txSz=0` (TX_4X4) for a 64x64 block: the tile driver's `txMode` takes the AV1 `TxMode` VALUE (0=ONLY_4X4, 1=LARGEST, 2=SELECT), NOT the raw frame-header `tx_mode_select` bit. The glue passed the bit, so `tx_mode_select == 0` became TX_MODE_ONLY_4X4. Mapping it (bit 0 -> LARGEST(1), bit 1 -> SELECT(2)) makes the frame read ONE block / one TX_64X64 transform and reconstruct **`Y=126` — EXACTLY ffmpeg's libdav1d output**. **This is the first END-TO-END pixel match on a real encoder's file.** **Proof (executed):** `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **6/6** (JVM/Script × plain + canonicalized + the two new solid-gray faces). **Next:** the same mapping on the complex real frame (testsrc), then the multi-block / chroma / frame-assembly composition and the `decodeRaster` AVIF route.
> **`known-bugs` §731 CATALOGUED 14/10 (diagnosis, nothing landed in libs — tree clean): the pure-Kof AVIF tile decode DIVERGES from the reference decoder on the SIMPLEST real frame.** A solid-gray 64x64 AVIF (one block, a 2-byte tile payload) reconstructs as `Y = 0,173,165` where ffmpeg's `libdav1d` reads a flat `126`. Every stage ABOVE the tile decode is pinned green against the real encoder's bytes (container, sequence header 3ay/3ba, frame header 3ax/3bb — the cfg is correct, `deltaQPresent=0`), so the divergence is inside the composed tile decode itself. This is the first end-to-end check against REAL bytes (earlier slices pinned stages on synthetic streams), and it fails at the simplest input; the reproducer is 2 bytes of tile, so it is cheap to drive. Recipe + evidence in §731.
> **AVIF slice 3bb LANDED 14/10 (pure Kof): the frame header EXPOSES the delta-Q/delta-LF facts the tile driver's `Av1ModeTail` needs — and the REAL ffmpeg frame reads `dq=0 dfl=0`, REFUTING the "wrong cfg" hypothesis.** `frameTailWalk` read (bit-counted) `delta_q_params`/`delta_lf_params` without carrying `delta_q_present`/`_res`, `delta_lf_present`/`_res`/`_multi` — the exact fields `Av1ModeTail` takes. `AvifFrameHeader` now exposes all five; the synthetic fixtures read them as `0` and `headerBytes` is UNCHANGED (the bit positions are identical). **The measurement that matters:** the real 64x64 ffmpeg frame reads `cdefBits=0`, `deltaQPresent=0`, `deltaLfPresent=0`, `seq enableCdef=1` — so the driver's `cfg` was ALREADY correct on those, and the pixel divergence is NOT a cfg problem. **Proof (executed):** `AvifFrameE2ETest` **16/16**, `AvifSeqE2ETest` **8/8**, `AvifObuE2ETest` **8/8**, `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **4/4** (the native faces now pass too — `known-bugs` §723 was fixed by the codegen lane). **Boundary (measured, NOT landed):** the Kof tile decode on the real payload still reads `Y0=129,112,122` against ffmpeg's `Y0=16,145,235` — the divergence is inside the mode-info/coefficient parse on a real tile, the next slice.
> **AVIF slice 3ba LANDED 14/10 (pure Kof + a REAL encoder's bytes): the item-data read of a real ffmpeg AVIF, unblocked test-side, PINS the real sequence facts — `enable_filter_intra = 1`, `enable_intra_edge_filter = 1`.** 3az recorded that ffmpeg's item is `[OBU_SEQUENCE_HEADER][OBU_FRAME]` with no leading temporal delimiter, which the Kof item walk requires. The fix needs no Kof change and no subset decision: prepending the delimiter yields the CANONICAL AV1 order with identical semantics — same real sequence header, same real frame, same real tile. On that canonicalized item the Kof walk reads `total=3 seqH=1 frames=1`, the sequence facts `bd=8 sub=4:2:0 fi=1 ie=1`, and the frame header `bq=160 ll=0 tx=1 rts=0` with the real 293-byte tile payload. **The finding that matters:** the sequence flags are `fi=1 ie=1`, NOT the `0` earlier passes assumed — the real sequence header was unreadable before, so every decode attempt fed the driver the WRONG filter-intra/edge-filter config. **Proof (executed):** `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **4/4** (JVM/Script × plain + canonicalized). **Boundary (measured, NOT landed):** with the correct flags the tile decode still RUNS (no throw) but its pixels do NOT yet match ffmpeg's — that reconciliation (the composition) is the next slice; the oracle is `ffmpeg -i x.avif -pix_fmt yuv420p -f rawvideo`.
> **AVIF slice 3az DIAGNOSED 14/10 (pure Kof + real file; nothing landed — tree clean): a REAL ffmpeg/libavif AVIF item begins with the SEQUENCE HEADER, not a temporal delimiter, and the Kof item-data OBU walk REFUSES it — this is the blocker between the 3ay walk and the pixel composition.** `avif_real_64.avif`'s item data starts with `0x0a` = `OBU_SEQUENCE_HEADER` (type 1) and never leads with a temporal delimiter (type 2); `AvifObu.avifItemObus` requires `type == 2` for the FIRST OBU (`IMAGE: avif item does not begin with a temporal delimiter`, pinned by `AvifObuE2ETest`'s `nodelim` error face). So `readAvifItemObus(path)` cannot yield the real sequence header (`enable_filter_intra`, `enable_intra_edge_filter`, bit depth) the tile decode needs — the composition can only fall back to the sentinel seq. **Why this is a GAP and not a silent fix:** the delimiter-first rule is a DELIBERATE, TESTED conformance choice (the `nodelim` face exists to keep it), so relaxing it is a subset decision — not something an agent flips alone. The two resolutions: (a) relax it for ISOBMFF items (accept `OBU_SEQUENCE_HEADER` first, update the `nodelim` face), or (b) require the sequence header in the `av1C` `configOBUs` (ffmpeg's `-f avif` puts it in the item data by default). **What IS landed from this pass:** the ffmpeg fixture + walk proof (3ay) and the exposed seq facts (`enable_filter_intra`/`enable_intra_edge_filter`, 3ay), so the composition is one decision away. **Measured, honest boundary:** the Kof tile decode RUNS on the real payload only when fed the sentinel seq, and its pixels do not yet match ffmpeg's — that reconciliation is the next slice, gated by this gap.
> **AVIF slice 3ay LANDED 14/10 (pure Kof + a REAL-FILE proof): the SEQUENCE header exposes `enable_filter_intra` / `enable_intra_edge_filter`, and a real ffmpeg-encoded AVIF pins the whole container→header→tile-group read path.** The sequence walk read (skipped) the two AV1 §5.5.1 flags behind `use_128x128_superblock` but never carried them, so the tile driver's `enableFilterIntra` and its intra edge filter control had NO source — the composition could only guess, and the guess was wrong (a real frame diverged). `AvifSeqHeader` now exposes both. **A REAL fixture:** `resources/avif_real_64.avif` is a 64x64 AVIF produced by ffmpeg's `libaom-av1` encoder through the `avif` muxer (the whole suite before this used SYNTHESISED boxes); it pins metadata, the frame header (with the slice-3ax `lossless`/`txModeSelect`/`reducedTxSet`) and the tile-group walk with its 293-byte tile payload against bytes Kof did not write. ffmpeg (`libdav1d`/`libaom-av1`) is ALSO the pixel oracle for the reconstruction slice. **Proof (executed):** `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **2/2** (JVM/Script); `AvifSeqE2ETest` **8/8** (the four fixtures' new `fi=0 ie=0` facts match the independent Java reader); `AvifFrameE2ETest` **14/16**, the 2 failures being the pre-existing `known-bugs` §723 native faces (A/B'd identical before this slice). **Boundary (measured, NOT landed):** driving the Kof tile decode on the real payload RUNS (21 blocks, no throw) but its pixels do NOT yet match ffmpeg's — the next slice; this is why the real-file test stops at the payload and claims no pixel result.
> **AVIF slice 3ax LANDED 14/10 (pure Kof): the frame header now EXPOSES the three tile-decode facts it computed and discarded — `lossless`, `tx_mode_select`, `reduced_tx_set`.** The tail walk (`AvifFrame.frameTailWalk`) already derived `CodedLossless` (base_q_idx == 0 and no non-zero segment qindex) and already SKIPPED the `tx_mode_select` (present only when not CodedLossless) and `reduced_tx_set` bits — but it returned only `{bits, baseQ, lf0, cdefBits}`, so the composition (`decodeRaster` AVIF) had no way to obtain the three frame-level values the tile driver takes as `lossless` / `txMode` / `reducedTxSet` without re-deriving them. The walk now reads and returns all three (`AvifFrameHeader` gains `lossless`, `txModeSelect`, `reducedTxSet`), keeping `headerBytes` unchanged (the bit positions are identical — the skip became a read). Spec: `CodedLossless` forces `TX_MODE_ONLY_4X4` (`tx_mode_select` absent), `read_tx_mode` = `TX_MODE_SELECT` when the bit is 1, else `TX_MODE_LARGEST`; `reduced_tx_set` forces `TX_SET_INTRA_2`. **Proof (executed):** the Kof probe, the independent Java reader (`AvifFrameJavaSupport`) and the golden all carry the new facts and agree — the three non-native faces (JVM/Script/Java-reader-agrees) **3/3**; `AvifFrameE2ETest` **14/16**, the 2 failures being `avifFrameHeaderOnNativeX86` + `avifTileGroupsOnNativeX86`, measured IDENTICAL on the pre-slice tree (pre-existing `known-bugs` §723, the register-locals default — not this slice).
> **AVIF slice 3aw LANDED 14/10 (test infra, pure Kof): the real interleaved tile's RECONSTRUCTED PIXELS pinned — the 3au "decodes end to end" claim now checks the pixel output, not only the mode records.** The slice-3au tree probe asserted the driver's per-block mode records and final entropy context but never read the reconstructed plane, so a wrong prediction/transform/plane-offset would have passed. The probe now also prints the four BLOCK_32X32 quadrant DC samples and the interior SUM, and the golden pins them (`P 128 128 128 128`, `S 524288`). The values are spec-correct: `DC_PRED` with no neighbours (`haveAbove == haveLeft == 0` → `1 << (bitDepth - 1)`) and a zero residual is flat mid-gray 128 at bit depth 8, so 64x64x128 = 524288. **Cross-target:** the tree face now runs on JVM + Script + JS (3/3) — the native faces of this probe are blocked by `known-bugs` §723 (the register-locals default), the same boundary the driver/partition native faces carry. **Proof (executed, hatch OFF):** `Av1PartitionE2ETest` **19/19** (17 → 19: the two new Script/JS tree faces); compile rc=0.
> **AVIF slice 3au RE-LANDED 14/10 (pure Kof): the tile driver is a CLASS and the walk DRIVES it — and `known-bugs` §705 is FIXED (the native x86 spin no longer reproduces).** 3au was reverted on 13/10 because its class extraction turned a CLEAN native bounds error into a SPIN (§705). Re-verified on the 14/10 tip: the reconstructed degenerate-stream repro (`data[0]=236` / `data[0]=11`, a 1-byte stream calling `Av1TileDecode.decodeBlock(0,0,9)` on a `BLOCK_32X32`) now EXITS CLEANLY (`rc=0`, `BEFORE | AFTER`) on native x86 — the fix came from the codegen lane's concurrent emission work (`D-CODEGEN-REWRITE` + Fase E ISA), not from any §705-specific change; the 16-byte stack-argument alignment padding was NOT the cause (measured: the minimal 21-arg repro is correct with AND without the pad). 3au re-landed byte-identical to `0f8ca55ff` (`Av1DecodeTileRecon.kf`, `Av1Partition.kf` + `bindDecoder`, the partition suite's `bindDecoder` test). **Proof (executed, hatch OFF):** `Av1DecodeTileReconE2ETest` **7/7** and `Av1PartitionE2ETest` **17/17**; compile rc=0. **Boundary:** with the DEFAULT register-locals path (`kof.native.register.locals` ON, `c044a2add`) the AVIF native faces still fail/stall — that is `known-bugs` §723, a separate open bug (a peer lane's regression), not §705.
> **AVIF slice 3au: §705 SEVENTH PASS 14/10 (pure Kof + test infra; nothing landed — tree clean): the 16-byte stack-argument alignment hypothesis is WEAKENED.** The sixth pass read ONE observation (a `pushq $0` pad made the hanging probe exit cleanly) as the cause. Two measurements this pass undercut it: a MINIMAL 21-arg reproducer (15 stack args, odd — the same shape as `decodeBlock`'s call) runs correctly `rc=0`/`21` BOTH with the pad and WITHOUT it, and the emitted runtime has no 16-byte-aligned SSE access to fault on (every SSE use is register-only `movd`/`movq`/`ucomiss`/`ucomisd`; `kof_alloc` is `lock cmpxchg` + `futex`) — a misaligned frame is benign here. What IS proven: the pad is ABI-correct (SysV) and zero-regression (driver face green with the register-locals hatch OFF; a 114-test native battery 114/114). It does NOT fix §723. Reverted again — the decision to land the pad as ABI hardening belongs to the compiler/native lane. See `known-bugs` §705 (seventh pass) and §723.
> **AVIF slice 3au: §705 FIFTH PASS 14/10 (pure Kof + test infra; nothing landed — tree clean): the hang IS INPUT-DEPENDENT (correcting the second pass) and 3au turns a CLEAN native error into a spin.** Four measurements on the emitted binary: (1) with `data[0] = 236` the native binary SPINS, with `data[0] = 11` the SAME binary exits cleanly with `Runtime error: array index out of bounds` — so the trigger depends on the entropy bytes and the second pass's "structural, not data-dependent" claim is **RETRACTED** (that claim came from a different harness); (2) the **pre-3au** top-level `av1DecodeTileRecon`, driven with the SAME 1-byte buffer and the same single `BLOCK_32X32`, exits with the bounds error (rc=1) where 3au spins — so 3au does not merely move the error, it converts a clean diagnosable error into a non-terminating spin; (3) forcing an in-`decodeBlock` bounds error (`above`/`left` of size 2) makes the 3au binary exit cleanly, so it is NOT "any runtime error inside a class method"; (4) with 256 bytes of data the 3au binary also exits cleanly — only the short/degenerate stream spins. **Together these point at one shape:** the spin needs the entropy reader to run out mid-block AND the exhaustion to land in the transform-block loop's later iterations — the interaction between the reader's exhaustion path and the loop, not the exhaustion itself and not the error path in general. **Also ruled out this pass:** an uncaught `throw` at top level exits cleanly (rc=1, message printed), and a `throw` from two levels deep in class methods (`Tile.decode` → `Reader.readSym` → `Reader.raw`) also exits cleanly — the native throw machinery is fine in both simple and nested cases. Next: reproduce with a two-block stream whose second block's reader is exhausted at a known symbol, and inspect `Av1Symbol.rawBits`'s exhaustion path in that call context.
> **AVIF slice 3au: §705 FOURTH PASS 14/10 (pure Kof + test infra; nothing landed — tree clean): the spin is in `decodeBlock`'s TRANSFORM-BLOCK LOOP, on the 3rd of 16 blocks, and my third-pass lead (`av1InvTx2d`) is RETRACTED.** Instrumenting the callees (prints + runaway guards, never inside the suspect call) gives a stable trail for one `tile.decodeBlock(0, 0, 9)` on native: `DB grid n=16 size=32 txSz=1 bw=8 bh=8`, then iterations 0 and 1 complete (`post-coeffs → post-avail → post-pred → DB post-recon` each), then `DB iter 2 of 16 → ordervals 0 4 → brbc 0 4 → (nothing)`. Markers INSIDE `av1DecodeCoeffsBlockEdgeTx`/`av1DecodeCoeffsBlockCore` fire for iterations 0–1 and NEVER for iteration 2 — so the spin is **in the 21-argument `av1DecodeCoeffsBlockEdgeTx` call**, after its arguments are computed correctly (`br=0`, `bc=4`) and before the callee's first statement. It is NOT `av1InvTx2d`: that function is never reached (it is called later, from `av1ReconstructBlock`), and instrumenting it showed it entered and returned cleanly twice. **Why the third-pass guess was wrong:** it read the `strace` pattern (repeated `futex(FUTEX_WAKE)` on `kof_alloc_lock`) as "a loop that allocates per iteration" and pointed at `av1InvTx2d`'s `new Int[w]` row loop — an inference from the syscall pattern, not a measurement of where execution stopped. The marker trail is the measurement. **A minimal repro of the call shape does NOT hang:** a class method with a `while` calling a 21-argument top-level function that prints and returns an `Int[]` runs all 16 iterations correctly on native, so arity alone is not the trigger. Cheapest next probe: print immediately before the `av1DecodeCoeffsBlockEdgeTx(...)` call AND as the first statement of the wrapper itself, to split "argument evaluation in the caller" from "the wrapper's forwarding"; if the wrapper's first statement fires, the spin is inside `av1DecodeCoeffsBlockCore` before its print (i.e. in `av1TxbCtx`, the first thing it calls).
> **AVIF slice 3au: §705 THIRD PASS 14/10 (pure Kof + test infra; nothing landed — tree clean): it is an INFINITE COMPUTE LOOP, not a stall, and the loop is AFTER `av1Dequant`'s rows.** The second pass left one ambiguity — "the function never returns" could be a deadlock or a spin. Settled by measurement on the emitted binary: `strace` shows the ONLY syscalls after startup are a repeating `futex(0x453820, FUTEX_WAKE, 0) = 0`, and `nm` resolves that address to **`kof_alloc_lock`** — so the program is ALLOCATING in a loop (each `kof_alloc` takes the lock with `lock cmpxchg` and releases it with a `FUTEX_WAKE`), not waiting on anything; and a clean single-process `ps` reading shows state **R** with CPU time accumulating and no output. **I also corrected a measurement error of my own:** an earlier `pgrep` had picked up an unrelated Electron process and reported state `S`/0 CPU, which would have supported the deadlock reading; and `pkill -f <name>` matched — and killed — my own shell, so exact-name `pkill -x` is required. **The assembly is NOT the culprit at the boundaries inspected:** `decodeBlock`'s 11-argument `av1ReconBlockInto` call site is correctly balanced (5 stack args, `addq $48, %rsp` after) and `av1Dequant`'s emitted loops have correct heads/increments/back-edges with a clean `movq %rbp, %rsp; popq %rbp; ret` epilogue. **Sharpest hypothesis to test first:** repeated successful `kof_alloc` means the runaway loop ALLOCATES per iteration, and `av1InvTx2d` (`libs/image/Av1InvTx.kf`) does exactly that — `var T = new Int[w]` inside `while (i < h)` and `var T = new Int[h]` inside `while (col < w)` — so a bogus large `h = 1 << av1TxHeightLog2(txSz)` there would produce precisely this. Note `av1Dequant` printed CORRECT bounds (`log2W=5 log2H=5 tw=32 th=32`), so if a bound is wrong it is the SECOND call to the log2 helper (the one `av1InvTx2d` makes), not the first. Next: instrument `av1InvTx2d`'s entry/exit and print its `h`/`w`/`log2H`, or hand §705 to the compiler/native lane; 3au stays reverted and the front continues with the tile-group/OBU layer, which does not depend on it.
> **AVIF slice 3au: §705 SECOND PASS 13/10 (pure Kof + test infra; nothing landed — tree clean): the hang is at `av1Dequant`'s EXIT, it is STRUCTURAL, and the original "46 locals" reading is now fully REFUTED.** New measurements, each from a print-marker run of the emitted native binary (marks emitted by instrumenting the CALLEES only, never `decodeBlock`): a single `tile.decodeBlock(0, 0, 9)` prints `BEFORE`/`AFTER` on the JVM and HANGS on native (one source, one input); the probe prints all 46 fields BEFORE the call and every one is correct; changing the input byte to `0` STILL hangs (structural, not value-dependent); the marker trail reaches `DQ in` → `DQ log2W=5 log2H=5 tw=32 th=32 denom=2 total=1024` → rows `i=0 … i=31` and then stops — the dequant loop COMPLETES and the hang is at its exit / `return out`; runaway guards inside both loops never fire (NOT an infinite loop); **removing the 46 `var x = this.x` hoists does NOT fix it** (frame size / local count RULED OUT); `ulimit -s unlimited` changes nothing (not stack exhaustion). **The load-bearing clue:** the pre-3au top-level `av1DecodeTileRecon` calls the SAME `av1ReconBlockInto`→`av1ReconstructBlock`→`av1Dequant` chain on the same targets and does NOT hang, so the callee is fine and the fault is in the CALLER/CALLEE frame interaction when `decodeBlock` is the caller. > **AVIF slice 3au DIAGNOSED further and CATALOGUED 13/10 (pure Kof + test infra; nothing landed — tree clean): the native x86 hang is a real cross-target divergence, now `known-bugs` §705.** Re-applied 3au locally and reproduced: the JVM/Script/JS faces pass, the native x86 binary SPINS with no output, and reverting to pre-3au makes the same face pass in 54–58s. **Narrowed by measurement:** a marked native probe prints `M1`…`M4 after-ctor` and stops at `M5 before-direct-decodeBlock`, so class construction and `planes()` WORK and the hang is a direct `tile.decodeBlock(0, 0, 9)` call; the arguments are right (`av1TxWidth(3)` → 32, `av1BlockNum4x4Wide(9)` → 8). **Inspected the emitted assembly (`KOF_KEEP_ASM=1`):** the prologue is `subq $992, %rsp` with all 46 slots written, the deepest slot `-720(%rbp)` is inside the frame, and a scan of the whole body finds ZERO offsets beyond the frame — so the earlier "the frame is too small for 46 locals" reading is REFUTED. **Four minimal native repros were REFUTED** (recorded in §705 so they are not repeated): many locals in a method, plain class-field access, a many-argument call inside a loop, and a method call on a field-held object inside a loop — all run. > **AVIF slice 3av LANDED 13/10 (pure Kof): the FRAME ASSEMBLY — and slice 3au REVERTED, because verifying the boundary I flagged last cycle caught a real native regression.** `libs/image/Av1FrameAssembly.kf` (new): `record Av1Frame(y, width, height, u, v, chromaWidth, chromaHeight)`, `av1StripPlaneBorder(buf, stride, w, h)` and `av1AssembleFrame(luma, frameW, frameH, chroma, hasChroma)`. The tile driver reconstructs into BORDERED planes (the chroma planes travel in the `Av1ChromaPlanes` bundle still carrying their one-sample border, `stride = width + 2`); assembly is where the border stops — it yields the flat planar frame the rest of `kof.image` consumes. The chroma extent comes from the bundle's own `wU`/`hU` (libaom derives the plane size from the block size and the subsampling, so it is NOT always `frameW >> 1`), and `hasChroma == 0` leaves the planes EMPTY rather than fabricating a plane the stream never carried. **Proof:** `Av1FrameAssemblyE2ETest` **6/6** (JVM/Script/JS/x86-64/riscv64/aarch64). No libaom oracle is claimed and that is stated in the suite: the assembly is pure ADDRESSING and the driver's output is already pinned against libaom by the driver suites; what these faces pin is the strip property those goldens cannot see — the probe fills every buffer with a POSITION-derived value, so a border read, an off-by-one or a wrong-stride read changes the number. **RED-first:** dropping the `+1` offsets makes the strip read the border and the JVM face fails (`100 101 102 105 ...`). **THE 3au REVERT (the important part):** last cycle I flagged the non-JVM driver faces as UNVERIFIED (the environment had killed those runs) and said they were the next cycle's first act. They were, and they came back RED: the native x86 driver binary HANGS with no output. Attributed by measurement, not by guessing — with the library reverted to pre-3au the same face PASSES in 54–58s, and restoring 3au makes it hang again. Bisected to `Av1DecodeTileRecon.kf` (new class + OLD partition file still hangs). Narrowed inside it: class construction and `planes()` work on native (a minimal probe prints `constructed` / `planes 64`), so the suspect is the extracted `decodeBlock`. Leading HYPOTHESIS (not proven): the extracted method carries ~46 locals (38 parameters + 8 derived) and the native prologue/frame handling for a frame that large is the suspect — the section 625/687 family. Per `AGENTS.md` (no-bug-ships; own red must be fixed or reverted) 3au is REVERTED and the finding recorded rather than shipped.
> **AVIF slice 3au LANDED 13/10 (pure Kof): the tile driver becomes a CLASS and the walk DRIVES it — the interleaved tile stream now decodes end to end.** The blocker recorded in 3at was structural: libaom calls `decode_block` from INSIDE `decode_partition` (`av1/decoder/decodeframe.c:1296`), so the partition recursion and the block decode must run in the same scope — and Kof has no closures, so a top-level function cannot be re-entered per block. The driver's per-block stage (mode info, tx size, coefficients, predict, reconstruct) moved out of the top-level `av1DecodeTileRecon` into a new `class Av1TileDecode` (`libs/image/Av1DecodeTileRecon.kf`), whose `decodeBlock(r, c, bs)` is exactly the old loop body and whose `run(blocks)` is the old loop; the top-level function is now a 3-line wrapper at the ORIGINAL 38-parameter signature, so every existing harness is unchanged. `Av1Partition` gained `bindDecoder(Av1TileDecode)`: with a decoder bound, the `decode_block` step of `addStep` decodes that block IMMEDIATELY — before the walk reads the next partition symbol, which is libaom's own interleaving and the only way the two stay in sync on a shared reader. **Proof:** `Av1PartitionE2ETest` **17/17** — the new `av1TileTreeDecodesRealInterleavedTile` walks the real interleaved stream and reproduces the oracle's OWN decode (4 `BLOCK_32X32`/TX_32X32/DC_PRED blocks + final entropy context 17). **RED-first:** making `bindDecoder` a no-op yields NO block records and an all-zero entropy context — the stream desyncs exactly as expected. **Behaviour preservation proved separately:** the extraction was validated against the existing driver tests before the new feature was added (JVM faces of `Av1DecodeTileReconE2ETest`, `Av1DecodeTileTxTypeE2ETest`, `Av1DecodeChromaE2ETest` all green), so the refactor and the feature are independently evidenced. **Next:** `av1AssembleFrame` — stitch the driver's planes into a frame; then the tile-group/OBU layer and `decodeRaster` AVIF.
> **AVIF slice 3at LANDED 13/10 (pure Kof + test infra): the SHARED READER seam and the FIRST REAL INTERLEAVED TILE STREAM.** `Av1Partition.useReader(Av1Symbol)` (plus `position()`) lets a caller bind the entropy reader it already owns. That seam is load-bearing and not cosmetic: libaom's `decode_partition` reads the partition symbol and then calls `decode_block` on the SAME `aom_reader` (`av1/decoder/decodeframe.c:1296`), so a real tile's partition symbols and block payloads are INTERLEAVED in ONE stream — a walk that must stay in sync with a block decoder cannot own its reader. Every harness before this one fed the walk a stream carrying ONLY partition symbols. **The new oracle** (`oracle4.c`, /tmp, not versioned): the same block encoder the 3ag/3t harnesses use, plus libaom's own partition helpers (`partition_plane_context`, `partition_cdf_length`, `partition_cdf_length`, `update_partition_context` over `partition_context_lookup`) writing PARTITION_SPLIT then PARTITION_NONE per quadrant, and a VERBATIM copy of libaom's `read_partition` reading it back — the stream is proven valid by libaom's own reader before Kof ever sees it. **Proof:** `Av1PartitionE2ETest` **16/16** (was 10) — 4 seam faces (behaviour-preserving: the walk bound to a caller-owned reader emits the SAME decisions as the constructor-owned one, on JVM/Script/x86-64/JS), 1 binding face, and 1 REAL-TILE face: the walk consumes an interleaved stream (`180, 72, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 16`) and yields PARTITION_SPLIT(64x64) then PARTITION_NONE(32x32). **RED-first, twice:** making `useReader` a no-op fails the binding face (the shared reader stays at its 15-bit seed while the walk's own reader reaches 24); the no-op still PASSES the 4 golden faces, which is exactly why the binding face exists — behaviour-preservation alone does not prove the seam binds. **An honest boundary:** the real-tile face asserts only the first TWO decisions. The walk has no block decoder to consume the payload between symbols, so it is in sync exactly until the first `decode_block`; closing that needs the partition recursion to call the driver per leaf (in Kof the recursion and the block decode have to share one class — there are no closures). **An oracle finding, NOT a Kof bug:** the directional-mode variant of `oracle4.c` desyncs (the decoder read 19 luma transform blocks where 4 are expected). Isolated by measurement — with all blocks DC_PRED the stream is self-consistent (4 blocks, TX_32X32, final context 17); the divergence is in the angle-delta path and lives in MY harness, so no golden was landed from that variant. **Lesson:** `mvn test` INCREMENTALLY compiles test classes and silently reused a stale `Av1PartitionSupport`; a clean recompile flipped the face from RED to GREEN. Recompile clean before believing a face.
> **AVIF slice 3as LANDED 13/10 (pure Kof): the BLOCK GRID the tile driver consumes — `av1TileBlockGrid` in `libs/image/Av1Partition.kf`.** The frame assembly needs `blocks` (the `(r, c, bs)` triples `av1DecodeTileRecon` walks), and the open question was who produces them for a REAL tile. **Measured:** libaom's `decode_partition` calls `decode_block` at every leaf of the partition recursion and nowhere else, so the walk's `kind == 1` steps ARE the mode-info blocks — the grid falls straight out of the slice-3o walk, no new decode. The glue filters those steps in decode order. **A correction I owe:** the recorded NEXT STEP said "use `qchroma.avif`'s real tile stream as the golden", and that fixture has NO tile payload — it is `delimiter + sequence header + frame header` only (`frameReducedQ` writes no tile-group OBU, and `containerWithItem` wraps just those bytes in `mdat`). Regression: frame 16/16, group 8/8, seq 8/8, metadata 8/8. Next: the glue — stitch `av1DecodeTileRecon`'s planes into a frame using this fixture's real tile stream as the end-to-end golden.
> **AVIF slice 3ap FIXED 13/10 (pure Kof): `known-bugs` §689 — the CFL alpha SIGN was inverted.** `readBodyBd` (`libs/image/Av1ModeInfo.kf`) negated the CFL alpha on `signU == 2`; libaom's `CFL_SIGN_TYPE` is `{ ZERO, NEG, POS }` (`CFL_SIGN_NEG == 1`) and `cfl_idx_to_alpha` returns `-(abs + 1)` for everything but `CFL_SIGN_POS`, so every `UV_CFL_PRED` block with a negative alpha decoded the OPPOSITE sign. > **AVIF slice 3ao ROOT CAUSE FOUND 13/10 (pure Kof, oracle-side; nothing landed — tree clean): the oracle/driver divergence was MY ORACLE's bug, and the Kof chroma reconstruction now matches libaom on 63 of 64 samples.** Printing what the driver DECODED for the CFL case's luma gave `N 3 256 9 0:1` — 256 levels, **tx_type 9 (V_DCT)**, one non-zero at index 0 with value 1. **The oracle had been using tx_type 0 (DCT_DCT)** because I captured `av1_get_tx_type` right after `kof_read_tx_size`, BEFORE the coefficient decode, and libaom only stores the type INSIDE `av1_read_coeffs_txb`. The chroma now differs in exactly ONE sample: the driver has `130` where the oracle has `128` at the block's first position; the other 63 of 64 match on both planes. Eliminated on the way, all by measurement: the dequant shift, the DC prediction (128 both sides), and last cycle's "the divergence is in the residual" reading — itself a consequence of the wrong tx type. **Next: the one-sample difference — both sides predict flat 128 and the AC comes from the same luma, so the +2 is either the DC predictor's first sample or the AC's first element; compare `ac[0]` on both sides first. Then re-add the raw `L`/`W` test and land the end-to-end CFL pixel golden.**
> **AVIF slice 3ao follow-up 2 — 13/10 (pure Kof, still NOT landed — tree clean): the degenerate hash is replaced by a RAW per-sample comparison, and the divergence has a SHAPE.** The rolling 16-bit hash returned powers of two (a degenerating multiplicative chain), so both sides now emit raw samples of the block area. For the CFL case (`ci == 3`: one BLOCK_16X16 at the origin, luma DC_PRED, DC-only residual) the ORACLE (libaom `idct.c` + `cfl.c`) reconstructs the luma as a FLAT **129** over all 256 samples — a uniform +1 residual over the flat 128 prediction, exactly what a DC-only transform gives. The KOF DRIVER reconstructs **128 in 255 samples with ONE sample at 137 (position 0)** — essentially NO residual. So the divergence is in the LUMA RESIDUAL and it is a SHAPE difference, now visible without a checksum. Attribution is STILL not established and this is NOT recorded as a Kof bug: the lone 137 could also mean the probe reads the block area at the wrong offset, or that the driver's luma path took another branch — the U/D/A records of the same case DO match, so the stream is in sync. **Next, cheapest probes in order: (1) print the Kof `levels` for that luma block (DC-only 74, or empty?) — `av1DecodeCoeffsBlockEdgeTx` already appends it to `levelsOut`; (2) print its `txType`; (3) only then look at `av1ReconstructBlock`/`av1Dequant` for a DC-only TX_16X16.** 
> **AVIF slice 3ao follow-up 13/10 (pure Kof, still NOT landed — tree clean): two candidates ELIMINATED, and the comparison INSTRUMENT is now suspect.** (1) The dequant SHIFT is not the cause: rechecking libaom's real body, `av1_get_tx_scale(tx) = (pels > 256) + (pels > 1024)` (`av1/common/idct.c`), and Kof's `av1DqDenom` (`libs/image/Av1Quant.kf`) equals `1 << av1_get_tx_scale` for every transform size — the tables agree (my earlier recollection of the thresholds was wrong). Setting the oracle to 129 moved its luma hash 4096 → 8192 and still did not match the driver's 31447, which is consistent. (3) WARNING: that rolling 16-bit hash returned POWERS OF TWO on this data — the signature of a degenerating multiplicative chain — so the mismatch reported last cycle may be a checksum artifact rather than a real divergence. **Next: replace the `L`/`W` hash with a sound comparison (sum of absolute differences, or a real CRC over the raw samples) and re-run BEFORE hunting a residual bug; if it survives, the divergence is in the RESIDUAL (dequant value or the inverse transform), since the shift and the prediction are excluded.** Then re-add the test and land the end-to-end CFL pixel golden.

Do NOT change `av1TxbCtx` (proven correct). Then

> **AVIF slice 3ah LANDED 12/10 (pure Kof, no new bug): the CHROMA transform size derivation (`av1PlaneBlockSize` + `av1GetMaxUvTxSize`, `libs/image/Av1IntraAvail.kf`).** libaom's `av1_get_max_uv_txsize(bsize, ssX, ssY)` (`av1/common/blockd.h`) is what `decode_reconstruct_tx` uses for every `plane > 0`: it takes the plane's own block size through `get_plane_block_size` (`av1_ss_size_lookup`), looks up its rectangular max transform and applies `av1_get_adjusted_tx_size` (which folds 64x64-and-larger down to 32x32). Chroma is NOT subdivided per block — the whole chroma plane of a mode-info block is ONE transform, unlike luma's per-block `read_tx_size` walk. **The trap this slice exists to close:** `get_plane_block_size` is NOT `scale_chroma_bsize` (`reconintra.c`, already in this library as `av1ScaleChromaBsize`). The former SCALES DOWN for the plane's extent (8x8 -> 4x4 under 4:2:0); the latter REMAPS the smallest blocks UP for the intra predictor (4x4 -> 8x8 under 4:2:0). Wiring the predictor helper into the coefficient path was my first attempt and the oracle caught it immediately — the two functions disagree on every subsampled size. **Oracle = libaom's OWN C tables** compiled into a standalone tool that calls `av1_get_max_uv_txsize` for every `(bsize, ssX, ssY)`: 88 combinations, 72 valid, ZERO differences; the 16 impossible ones (`BLOCK_INVALID`, where libaom's `assert` fires) are refused by name in Kof rather than silently indexing past the tables. The table transcription had two real errors (BLOCK_8X32/BLOCK_16X64 at 4:2:0), found by the oracle, not by inspection. **Proof (executed):** new `Av1UvTxSizeE2ETest` **8 run / 0F / 0 skipped** — JVM + Script + native x86-64 + riscv64(qemu) + aarch64(qemu) + JS re-emit all 72 golden rows, plus a refusal face asserting exactly 16 named refusals; RED-first confirmed (renaming the primitive fails the test with `SEM015`). Non-regression: `Av1DecodeTileTxTypeE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeTileReconE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeTileE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeTilePlaneE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeCoeffPlaneE2ETest` 8/8, `Av1DecodeBlockE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1TxTypeE2ETest` 6/6, `Av1TxSizeE2ETest` 6/6, `Av1IntraAvailE2ETest` 7/7 (121 run / 0F / 4 skipped). **NEXT STEP (the re-trigger reads this):** the chroma transform SIZE is now derived; the next chroma work is the chroma coefficient + reconstruction loop itself (per-plane `above`/`left` entropy context, the chroma tx-type via `av1ComputeTxType`, and the U/V reconstruction into the frame), then the tile-group/frame assembly, then `decodeRaster` AVIF. `decodeRaster` AVIF still refused until the chain closes.**

> **AVIF slice 3ag-c LANDED 12/10 (pure Kof, `known-bugs` §680 FIXED): the transform type is read BETWEEN `all_zero` and the coefficient levels, the order libaom uses.** The slice-3ag-b fix (`known-bugs` §679) wired `readTxType` BEFORE `av1DecodeCoeffsBlockEdge`, but libaom's `av1_read_coeffs_txb` reads the `all_zero` flag FIRST and calls `av1_read_tx_type` only when it is 0 — the type symbol sits BETWEEN the two. On any transform block with a residual the two symbols differ, so the driver read `tx_type` where the stream had `all_zero` and the entropy stream desynchronised from there: every later read (the next block's mode info and tx size) was garbage, which is what §680 recorded as a "wrong transform size for the 2nd and later blocks". `BLOCK_32X32` never showed it (`TX_32X32` signals no `tx_type`) and the §679 golden's one-byte tile had all-zero symbols, so the transposition was invisible — the same shape of mistake as §679, one level down. **Fix:** `Av1Coeffs.decodeBlock` split into `readTxbSkip(allZeroCtx)` + `setTxType(txType)` + `decodeLevels(dcSignCtx)`; `libs/image/Av1CoeffPlane.kf` gained `av1DecodeCoeffsBlockEdgeTx` (a thin wrapper over a shared `av1DecodeCoeffsBlockCore`) reading `all_zero` → luma `tx_type` → levels and stamping the type into the frame `txTypes` map; the driver calls it and reports the decoded type. The tx-size-context hypothesis §680 originally recorded was DISPROVEN (Kof ctx == libaom ctx on every block, including the failing ones). **Proof (executed):** `Av1DecodeTileTxTypeE2ETest` **7 run / 0F / 0 skipped** (JVM + Script + native x86-64 + riscv64/aarch64 qemu + JS) over a REGENERATED golden (`SHA256 5d40c024…`) whose case 1 is the four-block `BLOCK_16X16` plane that exposed the bug — block 0 (no residual) and blocks 1–3 (residual, types 2/2/1) all match the oracle. Non-regression: `Av1DecodeTileReconE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeTileE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeCoeffPlaneE2ETest` 8/8, `Av1DecodeBlockE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1TxTypeE2ETest` 6/6 (42 run / 0F / 2 skipped). **NEXT STEP (the re-trigger reads this):** the luma intra tile now decodes multi-block planes correctly; the next AVIF slice is the CHROMA planes (U/V through the same loop with the chroma transform sizes and the `compute_tx_type` derivation, whose non-DCT_DCT path is not yet exercised), then the tile-group/frame assembly, then `decodeRaster` AVIF. `decodeRaster` AVIF still refused until the chain closes.**
